\displaystyle-72\Psi\nabla^{m}h_{ij}\nabla_{m}\Psi-9\nabla^{m}h_{ij}^{(2)}\nabla_{m}\Psi-60h_{ij}\nabla^{m}\Psi\nabla_{m}\Psi+6\mathcal{H}\Phi\nabla_{i}B_{j}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\Phi^{\prime}\nabla_{i}B_{j}^{(2)}+\frac{3}{2}\Psi^{\prime}\nabla_{i}B_{j}^{(2)}+3\Phi\nabla_{i}B_{j}^{(2)^{\prime}}-12\Phi\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{i}h_{jm}-12h_{ij}^{(2)}\nabla^{2}\Psi

\displaystyle+12\Psi\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{i}h_{jm}+24\Psi\nabla^{m}\Psi\nabla_{i}h_{jm}+3\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{i}h_{jm}^{(2)}+3\nabla^{m}\Psi\nabla_{i}h_{jm}^{(2)}

\displaystyle-6B_{j}^{(2)}\mathcal{H}\nabla_{i}\Psi+12h_{jm}\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{i}\Psi+36h_{jm}\nabla^{m}\Psi\nabla_{i}\Psi-3B_{j}^{(2)}\nabla_{i}\Psi^{\prime}+6\mathcal{H}\Phi\nabla_{j}B_{i}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\Phi^{\prime}\nabla_{j}B_{i}^{(2)}+\frac{3}{2}\Psi^{\prime}\nabla_{j}B_{i}^{(2)}+3\Phi\nabla_{j}B_{i}^{(2)^{\prime}}-12\Phi\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{j}h_{im}+12\Psi\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{j}h_{im}

\displaystyle+24\Psi\nabla^{m}\Psi\nabla_{j}h_{im}+3\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{j}h_{im}^{(2)}+3\nabla^{m}\Psi\nabla_{j}h_{im}^{(2)}+6\Psi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\Psi^{(2)}

\displaystyle+3\nabla_{i}\Phi^{(2)}\nabla_{j}\Phi-3\nabla_{i}\Psi^{(2)}\nabla_{j}\Phi+3\nabla_{i}\Phi\nabla_{j}\Phi^{(2)}-3\nabla_{i}\Psi\nabla_{j}\Phi^{(2)}

\displaystyle+12h_{im}\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{j}\Psi+36h_{im}\nabla^{m}\Psi\nabla_{j}\Psi-3\nabla_{i}\Phi^{(2)}\nabla_{j}\Psi+9\nabla_{i}\Psi^{(2)}\nabla_{j}\Psi-3B_{i}^{(2)}\nabla_{j}\Psi^{\prime}

\displaystyle-3\nabla_{i}\Phi\nabla_{j}\Psi^{(2)}+9\nabla_{i}\Psi\nabla_{j}\Psi^{(2)}+6\Phi^{(2)}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\Phi+6\Phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\Phi^{(2)}+6\Psi^{(2)}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\Psi

\displaystyle+24h_{ij}^{(2)}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\Phi+12h_{ij}^{{}^{\prime}}+24h_{ij}^{{}^{\prime}}\Phi\Phi^{\prime}\Psi\Psi^{\prime}-12h_{ij}\nabla^{m}\Phi\nabla_{m}\Phi-12h_{ij}\nabla^{m}\Psi\nabla_{m}\Phi

\displaystyle-96h_{ij}\Psi\nabla^{2}\Psi-6B_{i}^{(2)}\mathcal{H}\nabla_{j}\Psi-3B_{i}^{(2)^{\prime}}\nabla_{j}\Psi-3B_{j}^{(2)^{\prime}}\nabla_{i}\Psi

\displaystyle+\text{(trace part)}\delta_{ij}+(hhh,\,\Psi\Psi\Psi,\,hh\Psi,\,\Psi^{(2)}h,\,\Phi^{(2)}h,\,B^{(2)}h,\,h^{(2)}h,\,\text{terms})\,,

(299)

whilst the third-order energy-momentum tensor reads

\begin{split}a^{-2}T_{ij}^{(3)}&=2h_{ij}^{(3)}P+6h_{ij}^{(2)}\delta P+6h_{ij}P^{(2)}+h_{ij}P^{(3)}-6h_{ij}P^{(2)}\Psi-6h_{ij}\delta P\Psi^{(2)}-2h_{ij}P\Psi^{(3)}\\
&+3B^{(2)}_{j}P\partial_{i}v+3Pv^{(2)}_{j}\partial_{i}v+3B^{(2)}_{j}\rho\partial_{i}v+3v^{(2)}_{j}\rho\partial_{i}v+12h_{jm}P\partial^{m}v\partial_{i}v\\
&+12h_{jm}\rho\partial^{m}v\partial_{i}v+3B^{(2)}_{i}P\partial_{j}v+3Pv^{(2)}_{i}\partial_{j}v+3B^{(2)}_{i}\rho\partial_{j}v+3v^{(2)}_{i}\rho\partial_{j}v\\
&+12h_{im}P\partial^{m}v\partial_{j}v+12h_{im}\rho\partial^{m}v\partial_{j}v+6\delta P\partial_{i}v\partial_{j}v+6\delta\rho\partial_{i}v\partial_{j}v-24P\Psi\partial_{i}v\partial_{j}v\\
&-24\rho\Psi\partial_{i}v\partial_{j}v+3P\partial_{i}v^{(2)}\partial_{j}v+3\rho\partial_{i}v^{(2)}\partial_{j}v+3P\partial_{i}v\partial_{j}v^{(2)}+3\rho\partial_{i}v\partial_{j}v^{(2)}\,.\end{split}

(300)

17.1
What source terms do we need to consider?

At third order in perturbation theory, there are then various products of first and second order terms which source third order GWs. This becomes obvious when we extract the TT of the third-order spatial part of the Einstein equation from the previous section

h_{ab}^{\prime\prime(3)}+2\mathcal{H}h_{ab}^{\prime(3)}-\nabla^{2}h_{ab}^{(3)}=6\Lambda^{ij}_{ab}S_{ij}^{(3)}\,,

(301)

We will now show that not all terms need to be considered when correlating third-order and linear tensor fluctuations.

For instance, the product of the first three-order scalar perturbations,

-8\Psi\partial_{i}\Psi\partial_{j}\Psi\subset S_{ij}^{(3)}\,,

(302)

does source GWs (i.e. it has a non-vanishing TT contribution). Nonetheless, when we consider
\langle h_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}^{(3)}h_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}},\lambda^{\prime}}^{(1)}\rangle
this term will not contribute since, as we said previously, first-order scalars and tensors do not correlate. Extending this logic to other cubic first order terms, we see that we need to only consider terms that contain two scalars coupled to one tensor or cubic first order tensor terms
29
29

29

From a perturbative point of view
hhh
will contribute as
\mathcal{P}_{h}\mathcal{P}_{h}
, whilst
h\Psi\Psi
as
\mathcal{P}_{h}\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
. Since
\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}>\mathcal{P}_{h}
, the
hhh
contributions will be subdominant. This was shown in Chap.
4
for induced second-order GWs for peaked input power spectra. We therefore ignore these contributions for the rest of this chapter. Source terms which include pure first-order tensors are shown in App.
D
. This line of thought extends to second-order perturbations. Schematically, we will therefore only consider terms that source
h^{(3)}
as:

S^{(3)}\sim h\Psi\Psi\text{ or }h\underbrace{X^{(2)}}_{\Psi\Psi}\text{ or }\Psi\underbrace{X^{(2)}}_{h\Psi}\,,

(303)

where
X^{(2)}
can be a second-order scalar, vector, or tensor. In the second-to-last term, we see that
X^{(2)}
can be sourced by two first-order scalars, while in the last term, it is sourced by one scalar and one tensor. To proceed, we require second-order equations of motion for second-order scalars, vectors, and tensors, sourced by first-order scalars and tensors.

Using momentum conservation, a closer inspection of second-order perturbations sourced by two scalars coupled to a first-order tensor shows that the correlation
\langle h_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}h^{(3)}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}\rangle
is proportional to

\langle h_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}h_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\underbrace{X^{(2)}_{\mathbf{p}}}_{\Psi_{\mathbf{q}}\Psi_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}}\rangle\propto\langle h_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}}h_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\Psi_{\mathbf{q}}\Psi_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\rangle\propto\delta(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\delta(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})\,,

(304)

which implies
\mathbf{p}=0
. Therefore, we see that we do not need to consider second-order scalars, vectors and tensors sourced by terms quadratic in first-order scalar fluctuations, when said second-order perturbations are sourcing third-order iGWs.

Bringing all of this together, the source terms in Eq. (
301
) we need to consider are

S^{(3)}_{ij}=S^{h\Psi\Psi}_{ij}+S^{h^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}+S^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}+S^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}\,,

(305)

where for clarity:
S^{h\Psi\Psi}_{ij}
is made of linear terms with one tensor perturbation and quadratic scalar perturbations,
S^{h^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}
a second order tensor with a first order scalar,
S^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}
a second order vector with a first order scalar, and
S^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}
a second order scalar with a first order scalar. We now proceed to give the exact form of these source terms.

17.2
Relevant source terms

In this subsection, we present in real space all the source terms that we need to consider throughout our work. These source terms have been simplified as far as possible, and we have extensively used the fact that perturbations are adiabatic and that the equation of state parameter is constant. For source terms which couple second-order perturbations to first-order ones, we also give equations of motion for induced second-order quantities, as well as their solutions in Fourier space.

17.2.1
Source term made of first-order fluctuations

There is one source term made purely of first-order perturbations. The scalar-scalar-tensor source is

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{h\Psi\Psi}_{ij}&=-4h_{ij}^{\prime}\Psi\Psi^{\prime}-\frac{1}{3(1+w)}h_{ij}\bigg(3(1+4w+3w^{2})\Psi^{\prime}\Psi^{\prime}+24(w^{2}-1)\Psi\nabla^{2}\Psi\\
&+(-17-16w+9w^{2})\partial_{m}\Psi\partial^{m}\Psi\bigg)-16h_{j}^{m}\Psi\partial_{m}\partial_{i}\Psi+12\Psi\partial_{m}h_{ij}\partial^{m}\Psi\\
&-8\Psi\partial^{m}\Psi\partial_{i}h_{jm}-16h_{jm}\partial^{m}\Psi\partial_{i}\Psi-8\mathcal{H}h_{ij}\Psi\Psi^{\prime}+\frac{8}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}\bigg((w-1)h_{ij}\partial_{m}\Psi\partial^{m}\Psi^{\prime}\\
&-h_{jm}^{\prime}\partial^{m}\Psi\partial_{i}\Psi+h_{jm}\partial^{m}\Psi^{\prime}\partial_{i}\Psi\bigg)+\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}\bigg((w-1)h_{ij}\partial_{m}\Psi^{\prime}\partial^{m}\Psi^{\prime}\\
&-2h_{jm}^{\prime}\partial^{m}\Psi\partial_{i}\Psi^{\prime}+2h_{jm}\partial^{m}\Psi^{\prime}\partial_{i}\Psi^{\prime}\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(306)

17.2.2
Source terms consisting of second-order scalar perturbations coupled to first-order perturbations

The source term which couples second-order scalar perturbations to first-order scalars, reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}&=\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}\partial_{i}\Psi^{\prime}\partial_{j}\Psi^{(2)\prime}+\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}\left(\partial_{i}\Psi^{\prime}\partial_{j}\Phi^{(2)}+\partial_{i}\Psi\partial_{j}\Psi^{(2)\prime}\right)\\
&+\frac{2}{3}\frac{(5+3w)}{(1+w)}\partial_{i}\Psi\partial_{j}\Phi^{(2)}\,.\end{split}

(307)

This term was considered in Ref.
[
206
]
in the context of third-order SIGWs, and our expression above is identical in an RD universe. To proceed, we need equations of motion for
\Psi^{(2)}
and
\Phi^{(2)}
, which was the focus of Sec.
12.1
.

From the trace Eq. (
165
), we are only interested in the scalar-tensor source term, which, after simplification, is

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{\Psi^{(2)}_{st}}&=2(1-3w)h^{ij}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\Psi\text{ .}\end{split}

(308)

The other equation, Eq. (
163
), obtained by extracting the symmetric trace-free part of the spatial second-order Einstein equations, encodes the difference between
\Phi^{(2)}
and
\Psi^{(2)}
, sourced from

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi^{(2),st}_{ij}&=12h_{i}^{m}\partial_{j}\partial_{m}\Psi-6(1+3w)\mathcal{H}h_{ij}\Psi^{\prime}+6(w-1)h_{ij}\nabla^{2}\Psi-12\partial^{m}\Psi\partial_{m}h_{ij}\\
&+12\partial^{m}\Psi\partial_{j}h_{im}-12\Psi\nabla^{2}h_{ij}\,.\end{split}

(309)

In Ref.
[
61
]
, the authors set
\Phi^{(2)}
and
\Psi^{(2)}
to be the same, since they focus on the IR limit of the iGW background.

To solve the third-order equations of motion, we then need the second-order equations of motion for
\Phi^{(2)}
and
\Psi^{(2)}
. We proceed to extract these in Fourier space. The second-order anisotropic stress equation sourced by first-order tensor and scalar fluctuations, Eq. (
163
), is given in Fourier space by

\Phi^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})-\Psi^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\frac{1}{k^{4}}\Pi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})\,,

(310)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})&=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\int\frac{d^{3}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\text{ }Q_{\lambda_{1}}^{\Psi^{(2)}_{st}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,k,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\,.\end{split}

(311)

In the above, we factored out the primordial values and defined for compactness

\displaystyle Q_{\lambda_{1}}^{\Psi^{(2)}_{st}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})

\displaystyle=k^{i}k^{j}\epsilon_{ij}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p}),

(312)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,k,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)=-12\left(k^{i}p_{i}-\frac{2}{3}k^{2}\right)T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)-12p^{2}T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\\
&+6(1+3w)\mathcal{H}T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}^{\prime}(\sqrt{w}\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+6(w-1)|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\\
&-12(k^{i}p_{i}-p^{2})T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+12\,k^{i}p_{i}T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\,.\end{split}

(313)

The second order trace equation for scalars, Eq. (
165
), in Fourier space is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&3\Psi^{\prime\prime(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})+3(2+3w)\mathcal{H}\Psi^{\prime(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})+(3w+1)k^{2}\Psi^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})+3\mathcal{H}\Phi^{\prime(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})\\
&-k^{2}\Phi^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\mathcal{S}^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})\end{split}

(314)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})&=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\int\frac{d^{3}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}Q_{\lambda_{1}}^{\Psi^{(2)}_{st}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})f^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\,,\end{split}

(315)

f^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)=-2(1-3w)T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\,.

(316)

We can now solve for
\Psi^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})
by making the substitution in Eq. (
310
) to get rid of
\Phi^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})
. This leaves us with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Psi^{\prime\prime(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})+3(1+w)\mathcal{H}\Psi^{\prime(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})+wk^{2}\Psi^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{S}^{st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})-\frac{\mathcal{H}}{k^{4}}\partial_{\eta}\Pi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})\\
&+\frac{1}{3k^{2}}\Pi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\sum_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}\int\frac{d^{3}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}Q_{\lambda_{1}}^{\Psi^{(2)}_{st}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})\Sigma^{st}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\,,\end{split}

(317)

where for convenience we defined

\Sigma^{st}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)=\frac{1}{3}f^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)-\frac{\mathcal{H}}{k^{4}}\partial_{\eta}f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,k,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{1}{3k^{2}}f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,k,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\,.

(318)

Eq. (
317
) can be solved via Green’s method. After applying the substitution
z=\Psi a^{\tfrac{3(1+w)}{2}}
and using the initial conditions:
\Psi^{(2)}(\eta_{i},k)=0
and
\partial_{\eta}\Psi^{(2)}(\eta_{i},k)=0
, we find the Green’s function

G^{s}_{k}(\eta,\overline{\eta})=\frac{(k\overline{\eta})(k\eta)\sqrt{w}}{k}\left(j_{b+1}(k\overline{\eta}\sqrt{w})y_{b+1}(k\eta\sqrt{w})-y_{b+1}(k\overline{\eta}\sqrt{w})j_{b+1}(k\eta\sqrt{w})\right)\Theta(\eta-\overline{\eta})\,,

(319)

which is valid for
w>0
and
b
we recall is defined in Eq. (
23
). The solution to the equation of motion for
\Psi^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})
is then given by

\Psi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\int\frac{d^{3}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}Q_{\lambda_{1}}^{\Psi^{(2)}_{st}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)h_{\mathbf{p}}^{\lambda_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\,,

(320)

where we have further defined

I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)=\int_{\eta_{i}}^{\infty}{\rm}\overline{\eta}\,\left(\frac{a(\overline{\eta})}{a(\eta)}\right)^{\frac{3(1+w)}{2}}G^{s}_{k}(\eta,\overline{\eta})\Sigma^{st}(\overline{\eta},p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\,.

(321)

17.2.3
Source terms consisting of second-order vector perturbations coupled to first-order perturbations

Another source term which we need to consider contains second-order vector perturbations,
B^{(2)}_{i}
, coupled to first-order scalars

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}\ &=-\Psi^{\prime}\partial_{i}B_{j}^{(2)}-\Psi\partial_{i}B^{(2)\prime}_{j}+B^{(2)\prime}_{j}\partial_{i}\Psi-2\mathcal{H}\Psi\partial_{i}B_{j}^{(2)}+2\mathcal{H}B_{j}^{(2)}\partial_{i}\Psi+B_{j}^{(2)}\partial_{i}\Psi^{\prime}\\
&-\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}\nabla^{2}B^{(2)}_{i}\left(\mathcal{H}\partial_{j}\Psi+\partial_{j}\Psi^{\prime}\right)\,,\end{split}

(322)

also considered in Ref.
[
206
]
and identical to ours. The second-order equation of motion for induced vectors is Eq. (
169
), where, after some simplifications using linear-order equations of motion, the source term is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{ij}^{B^{(2)}_{st}}&=-4h_{j}^{m}\partial_{m}\partial_{i}\Psi+4\partial_{m}h_{ij}\partial^{m}\Psi+4\Psi\nabla^{2}h_{ij}-4\partial^{m}\Psi\partial_{j}h_{im}\\
&+2h_{ij}\left((1+3w)\mathcal{H}\Psi^{\prime}+(1-w)\nabla^{2}\Psi\right)\,.\end{split}

(323)

In Fourier space, the second-order vector equation can be solved

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{r}^{\prime(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})+2\mathcal{H}B_{r}^{(2)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\mathcal{S}^{B^{(2)}_{st}}_{r}(\eta,\mathbf{k})\,,\end{split}

(324)

where the source terms read

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}^{B^{(2)}_{st}}_{r}(\eta,\mathbf{k})&=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\frac{i}{k^{2}}\int\frac{d^{3}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg(Q^{B^{(2)}_{st1}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})f^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+Q^{B^{(2)}_{st2}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})\\
&\times f^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\bigg)h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\,.\end{split}

(325)

We have defined

\displaystyle Q^{B^{(2)}_{st1}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})

\displaystyle=(k^{2}-k^{i}p_{i})k^{m}e^{j}_{r}(\mathbf{k})^{*}{\epsilon}_{mj}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p})+e^{j}_{r}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{j}k^{i}k^{m}{\epsilon}_{im}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p})\,,

(326a)

\displaystyle Q^{B^{(2)}_{st2}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})

\displaystyle=k^{i}e^{j}_{r}(\mathbf{k})^{*}{\epsilon}_{ij}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p})\,,

(326b)

and

\displaystyle f^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)

\displaystyle=-4T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\,,

(327a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)&=4(\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{k}-p^{2})T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+4p^{2}T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\\
&-2(1+3w)\mathcal{H}T_{h}(\eta p)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\\
&+2(1-w)|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}T_{h}(\eta p)T_{\Psi}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\,.\end{split}

(327b)

The general solution to Eq. (
324
) can be found via Green’s method (with initial condition
B^{(2)}(\eta_{i},k)=0
)

G^{v}(\eta,\overline{\eta})=\left(\frac{\overline{\eta}}{\eta}\right)^{2(1+b)}\Theta(\eta-\overline{\eta})\,.

(328)

The Green’s function for induced vectors does not depend on
k
as for the case of induced scalars or tensors, i.e. there is no propagating mode. The solution to the equation is then

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{r}^{(2)st}(\eta,\mathbf{k})&=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\frac{i}{k^{2}}\int\frac{d^{3}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg(Q^{B^{(2)}_{st1}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})I^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+Q^{B^{(2)}_{st2}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})\\
&\times I^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\bigg)h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\,,\end{split}

(329)

where

\displaystyle I^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)

\displaystyle=\int_{\eta_{i}}^{\infty}{\rm}\overline{\eta}\,G^{v}(\eta,\overline{\eta})f^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\overline{\eta},p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\,,

(330a)

\displaystyle I^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\eta,p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)

\displaystyle=\int_{\eta_{i}}^{\infty}{\rm}\overline{\eta}\,G^{v}(\eta,\overline{\eta})f^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\overline{\eta},p,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\,.

(330b)

Second-order induced vectors were previously studied in Ref.
[
137
]
.

17.2.4
Source terms consisting of second-order tensor perturbations coupled to first-order perturbations

Finally, the last source term couples second-order tensor perturbations,
h^{(2)}_{ij}
, to first-order scalars

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{h^{(2)}\Psi}_{ij}&=\left(h_{ij}^{(2)\prime\prime}+2\mathcal{H}h_{ij}^{(2)\prime}-\nabla^{2}h_{ij}^{(2)}\right)\Psi+2\Psi\nabla^{2}h^{(2)}_{ij}+(3w+1)\mathcal{H}h^{(2)}_{ij}\Psi^{\prime}\\
&-(w-1)h^{(2)}_{ij}\nabla^{2}\Psi+2\partial^{m}\Psi\partial_{m}h^{(2)}_{ij}\,,\end{split}

(331)

In an RD universe, our source term agrees with Ref.
[
206
]
. Second-order tensors sourced by first-order scalars and tensors were the focus of Chap.
4
, and all relevant equations can be found in Sec.
14.1
.

Having listed all the contributions at third order, we have also shown how the second-order variables are sourced from first-order perturbations. We now move on to solving the third-order GW equation for each source term.

18
Solution to the third-order induced gravitational wave equation

We now proceed to solving the third-order iGW equation, Eq. (
301
), in Fourier space

h_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}^{\prime\prime(3)}+2\mathcal{H}h_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}^{\prime(3)}+k^{2}h_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}^{(3)}=6\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}\mathcal{S}_{ij}^{(3)}(\eta,\mathbf{k})\equiv 6\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}^{(3)}(\eta)\,.

(332)

We can obtain the solution for the third-order tensor mode using Green’s method; the structure of the solution is identical to the case of second-order induced tensors

h^{(3)}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}(\eta)=6\int_{\eta_{i}}^{\infty}{\rm}\overline{\eta}\,\frac{a(\overline{\eta})}{a(\eta)}G^{h}_{k}(\eta,\overline{\eta})\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}^{(3)}(\overline{\eta})\,.

(333)

where the Green’s function was defined in Eq.(
182
).

In the following subsections, we present the solutions for each contribution in Fourier space. Since the solution to Eq. (
332
) depends on the equations of motion and solutions of both first- and second-order perturbations, we first provide a brief overview of how the corresponding third-order solutions are constructed.

For adiabatic initial conditions, first-order perturbations can be split up as Eq. (
124
) and Eq. (
132
) for tensors and scalars, respectively. However, a more complicated decomposition applies for second-order perturbations. While it is still possible to isolate the primordial value of the perturbation, one must also account for the non-linear evolution of the second-order variables. The time evolution of second-order perturbations is encoded in a kernel, as opposed to a transfer function at linear order, and so this will give rise to nested kernels. For this reason, in what follows, we distinguish between source terms made of cubic first-order fluctuations and the ones containing second-order perturbations.

18.1
Solution to the gravitational wave equation for the pure first-order contribution

The source term composed exclusively of first-order perturbations, corresponding to the coupling of quadratic scalar fluctuations to a tensor mode, given in Eq. (
306
). This leads to the following solution in Fourier space:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&h^{\lambda\,(3)}_{\Psi\Psi h}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,\,\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\bigg(\epsilon_{ij}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})I_{1}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)\\
&+16\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})p^{m}p_{i}I_{2}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)-8\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})p^{m}(q_{i}+p_{i})I_{3}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)\\
&+8\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})q^{m}p_{i}I_{4}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)\bigg)h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}}\,.\end{split}

(334)

Above, we have decomposed the first-order perturbations into their primordial values and transfer functions (and polarisation functions for tensors) according to Eq. (
124
) and Eq. (
132
). The factor of
\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}
comes from the TT projection operator, and we note that there is a sum over the two polarisation states of the first-order GW, denoted
\lambda_{1}
. Furthermore, we have collected all the time dependence into the kernels
I_{\textit{i}}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)
(
\textit{i}=1,2,3,4
), defined as

I_{\textit{i}}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\eta,k,p,q)=\int_{\eta_{i}}^{\infty}{\rm}\overline{\eta}\,\frac{a(\overline{\eta})}{a(\eta)}G^{h}_{k}(\eta,\overline{\eta})f^{\Psi\Psi h}_{\textit{i}}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)\,,

(335)

with

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\eta,k,p,q)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(-4T^{\prime}_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta p)-\frac{1}{3(1+w)}

\displaystyle\times T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)\bigg(3(1+4w+3w^{2})T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta p)-24(w^{2}-1)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T_{\Psi}(\eta p)

\displaystyle-(-17-16w+9w^{2})\,\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{q}\,T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T_{\Psi}(\eta p)\bigg)-12(\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{p}-p^{2})

\displaystyle\times T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T_{\Psi}(\eta p)-8\mathcal{H}T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta p)

\displaystyle-\frac{8(w-1)}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}\,\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{p}\,T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta p)

\displaystyle-\frac{4(w-1)}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}\,\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{p}\,T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta p)\bigg)\,,

(336)

\displaystyle f_{2}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\eta,k,p,q)=f_{3}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\eta,k,p,q)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T_{\Psi}(\eta p)\bigg)\,,

(337)

\displaystyle f_{4}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\eta,k,p,q)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(2T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T_{\Psi}(\eta p)+\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\times T^{\prime}_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T_{\Psi}(\eta p)-\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta q)T_{\Psi}(\eta p)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}T^{\prime}_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{\Psi}(\eta q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta q)-\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}T_{h}(\eta|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)

\displaystyle\times T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\eta p)\bigg)\,

(338)

We observe that the structure of these solutions remains largely unchanged compared to the case of second-order iGWs, except that there are now two unconstrained momenta,
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{q}
.

18.2
Solution to the gravitational-wave equation for source terms with second-order contributions

We now present the solutions for the source terms that involve second-order perturbations coupled to first-order ones. We begin with sources containing second-order scalars, then consider those coupling second-order vectors to first-order perturbations, and finally address the source terms involving second-order tensors.

18.2.1
Source term containing second-order scalars

The source term that couples second order scalars to first order scalars, Eq. (
307
), in Fourier space reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,\mathbf{k})&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{i}p_{j}\bigg(\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}\partial_{\eta}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\partial_{\eta}\Psi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\\
&+\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}\left(\partial_{\eta}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\Phi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})+\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\partial_{\eta}\Psi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\right)\\
&+\frac{2(5+3w)}{3(1+w)}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\Phi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(339)

We recall that second-order perturbations coupled to first-order scalar fluctuations will only contribute to the GW background if these second-order scalar fluctuations are induced by first-order tensors coupled to first-order scalars. We can write the above source term exclusively in terms of
\Psi^{(2)}
, via Eq. (
310
), which leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{i}p_{j}\bigg(\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}\partial_{\eta}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\partial_{\eta}\Psi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\\
&+\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}\bigg(\partial_{\eta}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\Psi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})+\frac{1}{p^{4}}\partial_{\eta}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\Pi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})+\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\\
&\times\partial_{\eta}\Psi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\bigg)+\frac{2(5+3w)}{3(1+w)}\bigg(\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\Psi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})+\frac{1}{p^{4}}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\Pi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\bigg)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(340)

The above source term is then substituted into the solution of the third-order iGW equation, Eq. (
333
), and we arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}h_{\lambda}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,\mathbf{k})&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{i}p_{j}Q_{\lambda_{1}}^{\Psi^{(2)}_{st}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})I^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)\\
&\times\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}h_{\mathbf{q}}^{\lambda_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\,.\end{split}

(341)

To arrive at the expression above, we have substituted in the equation of motion for
\Psi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})
, given in Eq. (
320
), the expression for
\Pi^{(2)}_{st}(\eta,\mathbf{p})
in Eq. (
311
) and split up the first order perturbations into their primordial values and transfer functions.
Q_{\lambda_{1}}^{\Psi^{(2)}_{st}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
is defined in Eq. (
312
) and there is a sum over the polarisation of the first-order GW since they are inducing the second-order scalars. The time dependence of the iGW is in the kernel,
I^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)
, defined as

I^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)=\int_{\eta_{i}}^{\infty}{\rm}\overline{\eta}\,\frac{a(\overline{\eta})}{a(\eta)}G^{h}_{k}(\eta,\overline{\eta})f^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)\,,

(342)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}T_{\Psi}^{\prime}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\partial_{\overline{\eta}}I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)+\frac{4}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}\\
&\times\left(T_{\Psi}^{\prime}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)+\frac{1}{p^{4}}T_{\Psi}^{\prime}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)+T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\partial_{\overline{\eta}}I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)\right)\\
&+\frac{2(5+3w)}{3(1+w)}\left(T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)+\frac{1}{p^{4}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)\right)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(343)

We now see explicitly that the third order kernel,
I^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)
depends on the second order scalar kernel
I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)
, defined in Eq. (
321
), giving rise to nested kernels. The function
f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)
is defined in Eq. (
313
).

18.2.2
Source term containing second-order vectors

In this subsection, we present the solution to the third-order gravitational wave equations, where the source consists of second-order vectors coupled to first-order perturbations.

The source term, given in real space by Eq. (
322
), in Fourier space reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}^{B^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=-i\sum_{r}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{i}e^{r}_{j}(\mathbf{p})\bigg(2B^{(2)st}_{r}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\partial_{\eta}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\\
&+2\partial_{\eta}B^{(2)st}_{r}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})+4\mathcal{H}B^{(2)st}_{r}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})+\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}p^{2}\\
&\times B^{(2)st}_{r}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\left(\mathcal{H}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})+\partial_{\eta}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\right)\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(344)

where we sum over
r
, the parity of the second-order vectors. After substituting the equation of motion for second-order vectors sourced by first-order scalars coupled to tensors, i.e. Eq. (
329
), and splitting up first-order perturbations into transfer functions and their corresponding primordial values, we can write down the third-order solution as

\displaystyle\begin{split}h_{\lambda}^{B^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,\mathbf{k})&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\sum_{r}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\,\bigg(Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})Q^{B^{(2)}_{st1}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{1}(\eta,k,p,q)\\
&+Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})Q^{B^{(2)}_{st2}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{2}(\eta,k,p,q)\bigg)\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\,.\end{split}

(345)

We defined a polarisation function

Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})={\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{i}e^{r}_{j}(\mathbf{p})\,,

(346)

whilst
Q^{B^{(2)}_{st1}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
and
Q^{B^{(2)}_{st2}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
are defined respectively in Eq. (
326a
) and Eq. (
326b
). The kernels are defined as follows, (
i=1,2
),

I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\textit{i}}(\eta,k,p,q)=\int_{\eta_{i}}^{\infty}{\rm}\overline{\eta}\,\frac{a(\overline{\eta})}{a(\eta)}G^{h}_{k}(\eta,\overline{\eta})f^{B^{(2)\Psi}}_{\textit{i}}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)\,,

(347)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f^{B^{(2)\Psi}}_{1}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(\frac{2}{p^{2}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{2}{p^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial_{\overline{\eta}}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)\\
&\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{4\mathcal{H}}{p^{2}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)\\
&\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(348a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f^{B^{(2)\Psi}}_{2}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(\frac{2}{p^{2}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{2}{p^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial_{\overline{\eta}}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)\\
&\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{4\mathcal{H}}{p^{2}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)\\
&\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)T^{\prime}_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(348b)

The nested kernels
I^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)
and
I^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\overline{\eta},p,q)
are defined in Eqs. (
330a
) and (
330b
), respectively.

18.2.3
Source term containing second-order tensors

Finally, the last type of source terms we need to consider contains second-order tensor perturbations.

The source term in Eq. (
331
) is expressed as a Fourier integral such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,\mathbf{k})=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{\sigma}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}{\epsilon}_{ij}^{\sigma}(\mathbf{p})\bigg(\big(\partial_{\eta}^{2}h^{(2)st}_{\sigma}(\eta,\mathbf{p})+2\mathcal{H}\partial_{\eta}h^{(2)st}_{\sigma}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\\
&+p^{2}h^{(2)st}_{\sigma}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\big)\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})-2p^{2}h^{(2)st}_{\sigma}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})+(3w+1)\mathcal{H}h^{(2)st}_{\sigma}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\\
&\times\partial_{\eta}\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})+(w-1)|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}h^{(2)st}_{\sigma}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})-2(k_{m}-p_{m})p^{m}h^{(2)st}_{\sigma}(\eta,\mathbf{p})\\
&\Psi(\eta,\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p})\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(349)

where we have summed over the polarisation states (
\sigma
) of the second order tensor. In the first line above, we recognise the second order equation of motion for tensors and substitute in the scalar-tensor source term and also substitute in the solution for scalar-tensor induced waves, Eq. (
230a
), in place of
h^{(2)st}_{\sigma}(\eta,\mathbf{p})
. We arrive at the third-order solution

\displaystyle\begin{split}h_{\lambda}^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,\mathbf{k})&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\sum_{\sigma,\lambda_{1}}\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}\epsilon_{ij}^{\sigma}(\mathbf{p})\epsilon^{mn}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{p})^{*}\epsilon_{mn}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{q})I^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)\\
&\times\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}h_{\mathbf{q}}^{\lambda_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\,,\end{split}

(350)

where the kernel,
I^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)
, is defined as

I^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)=\int_{\eta_{i}}^{\infty}{\rm}\overline{\eta}\,\frac{a(\overline{\eta})}{a(\eta)}G^{h}_{k}(\eta,\overline{\eta})f^{h^{(2)\Psi}}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)\,,

(351)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f^{h^{(2)\Psi}}(\overline{\eta},k,p,q)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(-8q^{2}T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{h}(\overline{\eta}q)T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)-8(p_{i}-q_{i})q^{i}\\
&\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{h}(\overline{\eta}q)T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+4\mathcal{H}(1+3w)\partial_{\overline{\eta}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{h}(\overline{\eta}q)T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\\
&-4(1-w)|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|^{2}T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)T_{h}(\overline{\eta}q)T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)-8p^{2}I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\\
&+4(3w+1)\mathcal{H}I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)\partial_{\overline{\eta}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)+4(w-1)|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|^{2}I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)\\
&\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)-8(k_{i}-p_{i})p^{i}I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)T_{\Psi}(\overline{\eta}|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(352)

The nested kernel,
I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{\eta},p,q)
, is defined in Eq. (
232a
).

This concludes our discussion of all terms and their solutions in Fourier space; we now turn to their contributions to the power spectrum.

19
Power spectra of the different third-order contributions

In this section, we compute the power spectrum for each contribution we have considered. In order to do this, we substitute in the third-order solution to Eq. (
294
) and correlate it with a first-order GW generated in inflation. Explicitly, we are after
\mathcal{P}^{\text{$(13)$}}(\eta,k)
, which can be found by inserting our third-order iGW equation solutions into

\langle h_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})h_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{(3)}(\mathbf{k^{\prime}})\rangle=\delta^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\delta(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k^{\prime}})\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\text{$(13)$}}(\eta,k)\,.

(353)

There are four different contributions, specifically:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{P}^{(13)}(\eta,k)&=\mathcal{P}_{\Psi\Psi h}^{(13)}(\eta,k)+\mathcal{P}_{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}^{(13)}(\eta,k)+\mathcal{P}_{B^{(2)}\Psi}^{(13)}(\eta,k)+\mathcal{P}_{h^{(2)}\Psi}^{(13)}(\eta,k)\,.\end{split}

(354)

In what follows, we have dedicated a subsection to each of these terms.

Beforehand, we recall that the power spectrum of the comoving curvature perturbation is defined in Eq. (
148
). Additionally, we again parametrise vectors in spherical coordinates and align the wave-vector
\mathbf{k}
(i.e. the wave-vector of the third-order iGW) with the
z
-axis (
\theta_{k}=\phi_{k}=0
). Moreover, we will see that the correlation constrains the ingoing wave-vectors to be
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}
(through conservation of momentum), and so it is useful to use the
v
and
u
coordinates defined in Eq. (
199
). For the nested kernels, it is useful to introduce
\tilde{y}=p\tilde{\eta}
. The subsequent relations are helpful

\quad p\overline{\eta}=v\overline{x},\quad|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}|\tilde{\eta}=\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\quad\text{and}\quad k\tilde{\eta}=\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\,.

(355)

We now proceed to compute each contribution. The results in this section hold for a general equation of state parameter
w\neq-1/3
.

19.1
The power spectrum of the scalar-scalar-tensor first-order contribution:

\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{\Psi\Psi h}

In this subsection, we compute the correlation of first and third-order tensor modes sourced by scalar-scalar-tensor interactions. We substitute Eq. (
334
) into Eq. (
353
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\langle h_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k^{\prime}})h^{\Psi\Psi h}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})\rangle=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\bigg(\epsilon_{ij}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})\\
&\times I_{1}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)+16\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})p^{m}p_{i}I_{2}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)-8\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})p^{m}(q_{i}+p_{i})\\
&\times I_{3}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)+8\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})q^{m}p_{i}I_{4}^{ssh}(\eta,k,p,q)\bigg)\langle h^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}}\rangle.\end{split}

(356)

The four-point function is broken down as

\langle h^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}h^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}}\rangle=\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda_{1}}\delta(\mathbf{k^{\prime}}+\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q})\delta(\mathbf{p}+\mathbf{q})P^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})P_{\mathcal{R}}(\eta q)\,,

(357)

where we recall that we are disregarding any connected contributions that could arise from inflation. The above suggests that, physically, out of the three modes responsible for inducing the third-order gravitational wave, one must share the same momentum as the outgoing third-order wave (or the first-order gravitational wave we are correlating with), while the other two modes must have opposite momenta, see Fig.
17
. This physical picture contrasts with the usual second-order production mechanism encountered in SIGWs, which was depicted in Fig.
6
. We now substitute the expression for the four-point function into Eq. (
356
) and integrate over
\mathbf{p}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle h_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}},\lambda^{\prime}}h^{\Psi\Psi h}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}\rangle&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\bigg(\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}\epsilon_{ij}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k})T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})I_{1}^{ssh}(\eta,k,q)\\
&+16\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k})q^{m}q_{i}T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})I_{2}^{ssh}(\eta,k,q)\\
&-8\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k})q^{m}q_{i}T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})I_{4}^{ssh}(\eta,k,q)\bigg)\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda_{1}}\delta(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k^{\prime}})P^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})P_{\mathcal{R}}(\eta q)\,.\end{split}

(358)

Using the normalisation condition of the polarisation tensors and the result

\epsilon^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}\epsilon_{jm}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k})q^{m}q_{i}=\frac{1}{2}q^{2}\sin^{2}\theta_{q}\delta^{\lambda\lambda_{1}}\,,

(359)

the two-point function finally reduces to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\langle h_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}},\lambda^{\prime}}h^{(3)}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}\rangle=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}q\int_{0}^{\pi}{\rm}\theta_{q}\int_{0}^{2\pi}{\rm}\phi_{q}\,q^{2}\sin\theta_{q}\bigg(T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})I_{1}^{ssh}(\eta,k,q)\\
&+8q^{2}\sin^{2}\theta_{q}T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})I_{2}^{ssh}(\eta,k,q)-4q^{2}\sin^{2}\theta_{q}T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})I_{4}^{ssh}(\eta,k,q)\bigg)\\
&\times\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda_{1}}\delta^{\lambda\lambda_{1}}\delta(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k^{\prime}})P^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{h}(k^{\prime})P_{\mathcal{R}}(q)\,.\end{split}

(360)

An important point to specify, relevant for source terms involving pure linear perturbations, is that once we have integrated over one of the internal momenta, there is only a trivial angular dependence remaining. This means that after expressing the remaining internal momenta in spherical coordinates, we can integrate over both the polar and azimuthal angles, as the only angular dependence will reside in the polarisation functions, see Fig.
17
for a geometrical interpretation.

\mathbf{k}
\mathbf{p}
\mathbf{q}
\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}
\mathbf{k}
\mathbf{k^{\prime}}
\mathbf{p}
\mathbf{q}
\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}

Figure 17
:
(
Left
). Momentum configuration of two scalar modes (dashed black) and a tensor mode (solid black), inducing a third-order tensor mode (solid blue). (
Right
). Geometrical configuration of the scalar-scalar-tensor two-point function when contracted with a first-order tensor mode. The two scalar modes must have opposite momentum, and the first-order tensor mode has equal momentum to the outgoing (third-order) one.

Furthermore, unlike second-order iGWs, the primordial tensor power spectrum appears outside the integral and does not depend on the modes inducing the third-order perturbations. Therefore, these contributions act as a modulation to the primordial gravitational wave power spectrum. By integrating over the angular directions in spherical coordinates, relabelling
\mathbf{q}
to
\mathbf{p}
, we can extract the power spectrum as follows

\displaystyle\begin{split}P^{(13)}_{R/L}(\eta,k)&=k^{3}\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{2}}P^{(11)}_{R/L}(k)\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}v\,v^{2}\bigg(\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{1}(x,k,v)+\frac{32}{3}v^{2}k^{2}\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{2}(x,k,v)\\
&-\frac{16}{3}v^{2}k^{2}\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{4}(x,k,v)\bigg)P_{\mathcal{R}}(vk)\,,\end{split}

(361)

where we have further defined

\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{\textit{i}}(x,k,v)=T_{h}(x)I_{\textit{i}}^{ssh}(x,k,v)\,.

(362)

Finally, the dimensionless power spectrum is given by:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{\Psi\Psi h}(\eta,k)&=3\left(\mathcal{P}^{R}_{h}(k)+\mathcal{P}^{L}_{h}(k)\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}v\,\frac{1}{v}\bigg(\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{1}(x,k,v)+\frac{32}{3}v^{2}k^{2}\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{2}(x,k,v)\\
&-\frac{16}{3}v^{2}k^{2}\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{4}(x,k,v)\bigg)\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{R}}(vk)\,,\end{split}

(363)

and the functions
f_{1}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\overline{x},k,v)
,
f_{2}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\overline{x},k,v)
and
f_{4}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\overline{x},k,v)
in the appropriate coordinates are

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\overline{x},k,v)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(-4k^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)-\frac{1}{3(1+w)}T_{h}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle\times\bigg(3k^{2}(1+4w+3w^{2})\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(c_{s}\overline{x}v)-24(w^{2}-1)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)

\displaystyle+(-17-16w+9w^{2})\,v^{2}k^{2}\,T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\bigg)-8\frac{k^{2}}{\overline{x}}T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)

\displaystyle+\frac{8(w-1)}{3(1+w)}\overline{x}v^{2}k^{2}\,T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)+\frac{4(w-1)}{3(1+w)}\,\overline{x}^{2}v^{2}k^{2}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{h}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\bigg)\,,

(364)

\displaystyle f_{2}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\overline{x},k,v)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\,,

(365)

\displaystyle f_{4}^{\Psi\Psi h}(\overline{x},k,v)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(2T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)+\frac{1}{3(1+w)}\overline{x}\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{h}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)-\frac{1}{3(1+w)}\overline{x}T_{h}(\overline{x})\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)+\frac{1}{3(1+w)}\overline{x}^{2}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)-\frac{1}{3(1+w)\mathcal{H}^{2}}\overline{x}^{2}T_{h}(\overline{x})\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}v)\bigg)\,.

(366)

19.2
The power spectrum of the second-order scalars coupled to first-order scalars:

\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}

In this section, we utilise the solution given by Eq. (
341
), which describes third-order gravitational waves sourced by second-order scalar perturbations coupled to first-order scalars. The corresponding two-point function is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle h_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k^{\prime}})h^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})\rangle&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})\sum_{\lambda_{1}}{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{i}p_{j}Q^{\lambda_{1}}_{\pi,st}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\\
&\times I^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)\langle h^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}h_{\mathbf{q}}^{\lambda_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\rangle\,.\end{split}

(367)

The four-point function can be broken down as

\langle h^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}h_{\mathbf{q}}^{\lambda_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\rangle=\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda_{1}}\delta(\mathbf{k^{\prime}}+\mathbf{q})\delta(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q})P^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{h}(k^{\prime})P_{\mathcal{R}}(|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}|)\,,

(368)

so that we can integrate over
\mathbf{q}
to arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle h_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}},\lambda^{\prime}}h^{(3)}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}\rangle&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})\sum_{\lambda_{1}}{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{i}p_{j}Q^{\lambda_{1}}_{\pi,st}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k})I^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q=k)\\
&\times\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda_{1}}\delta(\mathbf{k^{\prime}}+\mathbf{k})P^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{h}(k^{\prime})P_{\mathcal{R}}(|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{k}|)\,.\end{split}

(369)

After switching to spherical coordinates, we can integrate the polarisation functions over the azimuthal direction

\int{\rm}\phi_{p}\,{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}p_{i}p_{j}Q_{\lambda_{1}}^{\Pi^{(2)}_{st}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{k})=\frac{\pi}{2}k^{4}v^{4}\left(1-\frac{(1-u^{2}+v^{2})^{2}}{4v^{2}}\right)^{2}\delta^{\lambda\lambda_{1}}\,,

(370)

where we have switched to
v
and
u
coordinates. As opposed to the source term made of pure first-order perturbations, the
u
dependence is now non-trivial (see Fig.
18
) and this will be the case for all the source terms involving second-order perturbations we consider.

\mathbf{k}
\mathbf{q}
\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}
\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}
\mathbf{p}
\mathbf{k}
\mathbf{k^{\prime}}
\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}
\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}
\mathbf{q}

Figure 18
:
(
Left
). Momentum configuration of a second-order mode (solid red) and a scalar mode (dashed black), inducing a third-order tensor mode (solid blue). The second-order perturbation is itself induced by a scalar and a first-order tensor perturbation (solid black). (
Right
). Geometrical configuration of the two-point function resulting from a second-order perturbation coupled to a first-order scalar perturbation with a first-order tensor mode. In this configuration, the two scalar modes depend on the angle between the incoming second-order mode and the outgoing third-order mode, making the dependence on the coordinate
u
non-trivial.

The power spectrum can now be obtained and is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}P^{(13)}_{R/L}&=\frac{3}{2^{3}\pi^{2}}k^{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}v\int_{|1-v|}^{1+v}{\rm}u\,uv\,k^{4}\mathcal{I}^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)P^{(11)}_{R/L}(k)P_{\mathcal{R}}(ku)\\
&\times\left(v^{2}-\frac{(1-u^{2}+v^{2})^{2}}{4}\right)^{2}\,,\end{split}

(371)

where we have defined

\mathcal{I}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)=T_{h}(x)I^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)\,,

(372)

which takes into account the sub-Hubble evolution of the tensor mode we have correlated with. Finally, the dimensionless power spectrum is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k)&=\frac{3}{4}\left(\mathcal{P}^{(11)}_{R}(\eta,k)+\mathcal{P}^{(11)}_{L}(\eta,k)\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}v\int_{|1-v|}^{1+v}{\rm}u\,\frac{v}{u^{2}}k^{4}\mathcal{I}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)\\
&\times\left(v^{2}-\frac{(1-u^{2}+v^{2})^{2}}{4}\right)^{2}P_{\mathcal{R}}(ku)\,.\end{split}

(373)

We emphasise once again that the kernel and the primordial scalar power spectrum exhibit angular dependence, making the polar integral (the integral over
u
) non-trivial to evaluate—unlike the case of third-order waves sourced purely by first-order contributions, where the integration is trivial. The kernel
I^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)
was defined in Eq. (
342
) and we now give
f^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{x},k,v,u)
in terms of
v
and
u

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{x},k,v,u)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(\frac{(1+3w)^{2}}{3(1+w)}\overline{x}^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\\
&+\frac{2(1+3w)}{3(1+w)}\overline{x}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)+\frac{1}{k^{4}v^{4}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\\
&+T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\bigg)+\frac{2(5+3w)}{3(1+w)}\bigg(T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\\
&+\frac{1}{k^{4}v^{4}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\bigg)\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(374)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}f^{\Pi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)&=2k^{2}(1+3u^{2}-3v^{2})T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)-12k^{2}T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\\
&+12\frac{k^{2}}{\overline{x}}T_{h}(\overline{x})\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)+6(w-1)k^{2}u^{2}T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\\
&+6k^{2}(1+u^{2}-v^{2})T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)+6k^{2}(1-u^{2}+v^{2})T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\,,\end{split}

(375)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)&=\frac{\sqrt{w}}{\overline{x}^{1+b}k^{2}v}\int_{0}^{\overline{x}v}{\rm}\tilde{y}\,\,\tilde{y}^{3+b}\big(j_{b+1}(\tilde{y}\sqrt{w})y_{b+1}(v\overline{x}\sqrt{w})-y_{b+1}(\tilde{y}\sqrt{w})\\
&\times j_{b+1}(v\overline{x}\sqrt{w})\big)\Sigma^{st}(\tilde{y},v,u)\,.\end{split}

(376)

We have defined
\Sigma^{st}(\tilde{y},v,u)
in Eq. (
318
) and it now reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Sigma^{st}(\tilde{y},v,u)=\frac{2}{3(1+3w)v^{3}\tilde{y}^{3}}\bigg(6\tilde{y}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)\bigg((3+v^{2}-3u^{2}w)\tilde{y}T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)-6uv\\
&\times\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)\bigg)+T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)\bigg[v(1+3w)(-3-2v^{2}+3(u^{2}+v^{2})w)\tilde{y}^{3}T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)\\
&+6u\bigg(\big[6v^{2}+(3-3u^{2}w+v^{2}(2+3w))\tilde{y}^{2}\big]\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)-6uv\tilde{y}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\tilde{y}^{2}}T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)\bigg)\bigg]\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(377)

where we have used the relations shown in Eq. (
355
).

19.3
The power spectrum of second-order vectors coupled to first-order scalars:

\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{B^{(2)}\Psi}

In this subsection, we compute the contribution coming from second-order vectors coupled to first-order scalars. This amounts to substituting in the solution Eq. (
345
) to the two-point function,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle h_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k^{\prime}})h^{B^{(2)\Psi}}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})\rangle&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})\sum_{r}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\bigg(Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})Q^{B^{(2)}_{st1}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\\
&\times I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{1}(\eta,k,p,q)+Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})Q^{B^{(2)}_{st2}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{2}(\eta,k,p,q)\bigg)\\
&\times\langle h^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}h_{\mathbf{q}}^{\lambda_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\rangle\,.\end{split}

(378)

Inserting the expression for the four-point function given in Eq. (
368
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\langle h_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k^{\prime}})h^{B^{(2)\Psi}}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})\rangle=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})\sum_{r}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}\bigg(Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})Q^{B^{(2)}_{st1}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{k})\\
&\times I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{1}(\eta,k,p,q=k)+Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})Q^{B^{(2)}_{st2}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{k})I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{2}(\eta,k,p,q=k)\bigg)\\
&\times\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda_{1}}\delta(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k^{\prime}})P^{(11)}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(k)P_{\mathcal{R}}(|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{k}|)\,.\end{split}

(379)

We can now integrate over
\mathbf{q}
and perform the azimuthal integral of the wave-vector
\mathbf{p}
over the polarisation functions

\displaystyle\int{\rm}\phi_{p}\,\sum_{r}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})Q^{B^{(2)}_{st1}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=-\frac{k^{4}\pi}{32v^{2}}a^{B^{(2)}\Psi}(v,u)b^{B^{(2)}\Psi}(v,u)\delta^{\lambda\lambda_{1}}\,,

(380a)

\displaystyle\int{\rm}\phi_{p}\,\sum_{r}\sum_{\lambda_{1}}Q^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\lambda,r}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{p})Q^{B^{(2)}_{st2}}_{r,\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=-\frac{k^{2}\pi}{32v^{2}}a^{B^{(2)}\Psi}(v,u)c^{B^{(2)}\Psi}(v,u)\delta^{\lambda\lambda_{1}}\,,

(380b)

where we have further defined

\displaystyle a^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)

\displaystyle=(-1+u-v)(1+u-v)(-1+u+v)(1+u+v)\,,

(381a)

\displaystyle b^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)

\displaystyle=-3+u^{4}+2v^{2}+v^{4}-2u^{2}(v^{2}-1)\,,

(381b)

\displaystyle c^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)

\displaystyle=1+u^{4}+6v^{2}+v^{4}-2u^{2}(1+v^{2})\,.

(381c)

The power spectrum is then given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}P^{(13)}_{R/L}&=\frac{3}{(2\pi)^{2}}k^{3}P^{(11)}_{R/L}(k)\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}v\int_{|1-v|}^{1+v}{\rm}u\,uv\bigg(-\frac{k^{4}}{32v^{2}}a^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)b^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)\\
&\times\mathcal{I}^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{1}(x,k,v,u)-\frac{k^{2}}{32v^{2}}a^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)c^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)\mathcal{I}^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{2}(x,k,v,u)\bigg)P_{\mathcal{R}}(ku)\,,\end{split}

(382)

with

\mathcal{I}^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\textit{i}}(x,k,v,u)=T_{h}(x)I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\textit{i}}(x,k,v,u)\,.

(383)

Finally, the dimensionless power spectrum corresponding to iGWs sourced by second-order vectors coupled to first-order scalar perturbations is given by:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{B^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k)&=-\frac{3}{64}\left(\mathcal{P}^{(11)}_{R}(\eta,k)+\mathcal{P}^{(11)}_{L}(\eta,k)\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}v\int_{|1-v|}^{1+v}{\rm}u\,\frac{1}{vu^{2}}\\
&\times\bigg(a^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)b^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)k^{4}\mathcal{I}^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{1}(x,k,v,u)+a^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)c^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(v,u)\\
&\times k^{2}\mathcal{I}^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{2}(x,k,v,u)\bigg)P_{\mathcal{R}}(ku)\,.\end{split}

(384)

The two kernel’s
I^{B^{(2)}\Psi}_{\textit{i}}(x,k,v,u)
, defined in Eq. (
347
) are time integrals over the functions

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f^{B^{(2)\Psi}}_{\textit{i}}(\overline{x},k,p,q)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(\frac{2}{v^{2}k}I^{B^{(2)}}_{sti}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)+\frac{2}{v^{2}k}\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{sti}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\\
&\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)+\frac{8}{v^{2}k\overline{x}(1+3w)}I^{B^{(2)}}_{sti}(\overline{x},k,v,u)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)+\frac{\overline{x}(1+3w)}{6k(1+w)}I^{B^{(2)}}_{sti}(\overline{x},k,v,u)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\\
&+\frac{\overline{x}^{2}(1+3w)^{2}}{12k(1+w)}I^{B^{(2)}}_{sti}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(385a)

The expression for the nested kernels (
i=1,2
) are

I^{B^{(2)}}_{sti}(\overline{x},k,v,u)=\frac{1}{(kv)^{3+2b}\overline{x}^{2(1+b)}}\int_{0}^{\overline{x}v}{\rm}\tilde{y}\,\,\tilde{y}^{2(1+b)}f^{B^{(2)}}_{sti}(\tilde{y},k,v,u)\,,

(386)

with

\displaystyle f^{B^{(2)}}_{st1}(\tilde{y},k,v,u)

\displaystyle=-4T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)\,,

(387a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f^{B^{(2)}}_{st2}(\tilde{y},k,v,u)&=2k^{2}(-1+v^{2}-u^{2})T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)+4k^{2}T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)\\
&-\frac{4v^{2}k^{2}}{\tilde{y}}T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)+2(1-w)u^{2}k^{2}T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)\,.\end{split}

(387b)

19.4
The power spectrum of second-order tensors coupled to first-order scalars:

\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{h^{(2)}\Psi}

The last term to consider is the two-point function involving third-order iGWs sourced by second-order tensors and first-order scalars. The equation of motion for these iGWs was derived in Eq. (
350
). So we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle h_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k^{\prime}})h^{h^{(2)\Psi}}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})\rangle&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{q}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}\,T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})\sum_{\sigma,\lambda_{1}}{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}{\epsilon}_{ij}^{\sigma}(\mathbf{p}){\epsilon}^{mn}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{p})^{*}{\epsilon}_{mn}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{q})\\
&\times I_{st}^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q)\langle h^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}}}h_{\mathbf{q}}^{\lambda_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{p}}\rangle\,,\end{split}

(388)

and following the same steps outlined in the previous subsections, we arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle h_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}},\lambda^{\prime}}h^{(3)}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}\rangle&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int{}^{3}\mathbf{p}T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})\sum_{\sigma,\lambda_{1}}{\epsilon}^{ij}_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k})^{*}{\epsilon}_{ij}^{\sigma}(\mathbf{p}){\epsilon}^{mn}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{p})^{*}{\epsilon}_{mn}^{\lambda_{1}}(\mathbf{k})I_{st}^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q=k)\\
&\times\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda_{1}}\delta(\mathbf{k^{\prime}}+\mathbf{k})P^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{h}(k^{\prime})P_{\mathcal{R}}(|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{k}|)\,.\end{split}

(389)

Contracting the polarisation tensors and integrating over the azimuthal direction, the only non-trivial contribution happens when
\lambda=\lambda_{1}
, which leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle h_{\mathbf{k^{\prime}},\lambda^{\prime}}h^{(3)}_{\mathbf{k},\lambda}\rangle&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}p\int_{0}^{\pi}{\rm}\theta_{p}\,p^{2}\sin\theta_{p}\left(\cos{\left(\frac{\theta_{p}}{2}\right)}^{8}+\sin{\left(\frac{\theta_{p}}{2}\right)}^{8}\right)\\
&\times T_{h}(\eta k^{\prime})I^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k,p,q=k)\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda}\delta(\mathbf{k^{\prime}}+\mathbf{k})P^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{h}(k^{\prime})P_{\mathcal{R}}(|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{k}|)\,.\end{split}

(390)

Switching to the (
v,u
) coordinate system, the power spectrum is now given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}P^{(13)}_{R/L}(\eta,k)&=\frac{6}{(2\pi)^{2}}k^{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}v\int_{|1-v|}^{1+v}{\rm}u\,\frac{u}{256v^{3}}\big[\left(u^{2}-(v+1)^{2}\right)^{4}+\left(u^{2}-(v-1)^{2}\right)^{4}\big]\\
&\times\mathcal{I}^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)P^{(11)}_{R/L}(k)P_{\mathcal{R}}(ku)\,,\end{split}

(391)

with the kernel defined as

\mathcal{I}^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)=T_{h}(x)I^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)\,.

(392)

Finally, the dimensionless power spectrum reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k)&=\frac{3}{128}\left(\mathcal{P}^{R}_{h}(\eta,k)+\mathcal{P}^{L}_{h}(\eta,k)\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm}v\int_{|1-v|}^{1+v}{\rm}u\,\frac{1}{v^{3}u^{2}}\bigg[\left(u^{2}-(v+1)^{2}\right)^{4}\\
&+\left(u^{2}-(v-1)^{2}\right)^{4}\bigg]\mathcal{I}^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)P_{\mathcal{R}}(ku)\,.\end{split}

(393)

The kernel
I^{h^{(2)}\Psi}(x,k,v,u)
, defined in Eq. (
392
), is an integral over the function
f^{h^{(2)\Psi}}(\overline{x},k,v,u)
, which was previously defined in Eq. (
352
). In our new coordinates, it is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f^{h^{(2)\Psi}}(\overline{x},k,v,u)=\left(\frac{3+3w}{5+3w}\right)^{2}\bigg(-8k^{2}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)+4k^{2}(1+u^{2}-v^{2})\\
&\times T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)+4\frac{k^{2}}{\overline{x}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\\
&-4(1-w)k^{2}u^{2}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)T_{h}(\overline{x})T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)-8v^{2}k^{2}I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\\
&+8\frac{k^{2}}{\overline{x}}I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)+4(w-1)k^{2}u^{2}I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\\
&+4k^{2}(-1+u^{2}-v^{2})I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)T_{\Psi}(\overline{x}u)\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(394)

Finally, the nested kernels can be expressed as

\displaystyle\begin{split}I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)&=\frac{v^{b-2}}{\overline{x}^{b}k^{2}}\int_{0}^{\overline{x}v}{\rm}\tilde{y}\,\,\tilde{y}^{2+b}\big(j_{b}(\tilde{y})y_{b}(v\overline{x})-y_{b}(\tilde{y})j_{b}(v\overline{x})\big)f^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\tilde{y},k,v,u)\,,\end{split}

(395)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}f^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\tilde{y},k,v,u)&=-2k^{2}T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)+k^{2}(1+u^{2}-v^{2})T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)\\
&+2\frac{v^{2}k^{2}}{\tilde{y}}T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)-k^{2}u^{2}(1-w)T_{h}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{v}\right)T_{\Psi}\left(\frac{\tilde{y}u}{v}\right)\,.\end{split}

(396)

20
Results in a radiation-dominated universe for a peaked input power spectrum

In the previous section, we derived expressions for the various contributions to the power spectrum arising from the correlation between third-order iGWs and first-order GWs. Our results, so far, hold for a general constant equation of state
w\neq-1/3
. We now proceed to derive the spectral density of these GWs in an RD universe, setting
w=1/3
or
b=0
henceforth. The spectral density of the iGWs, at the time of creation, defined in Eq. (
298
), consists of taking the time average of the power spectrum arising from the different contributions

\Omega^{(13)}(\eta,k)=\frac{1}{9}x^{2}\left(\overline{\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{\Psi\Psi h}(\eta,k)}+\overline{\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k)}+\overline{\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{B^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k)}+\overline{\mathcal{P}^{(13)}_{h^{(2)}\Psi}(\eta,k)}\right)\,.

(397)

We therefore need to take the time average of the power spectra we have derived. Since the time dependence is entirely contained within the kernels, our task now is to compute their time-averaged values, which we will do in this section. As we will see, terms involving nested kernels will need to be computed numerically; however, some terms can still be evaluated analytically. Hence, where possible, we will split kernels into an analytical and a numerical part.

We recall that the explicit forms of the transfer functions are given in Eq. (
132
) for the first-order scalar fluctuations and Eq. (
125
) for the tensor modes. Furthermore, the Green’s function for the second-order tensor equation can be found in Eq. (
182
).

The kernels we have defined for each contribution,
\mathcal{I}^{i}(x,k,v,u)
(here
i
refers to the type of source), can be expanded as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{I}^{i}(x,k,v,u)=T_{h}(x)I^{i}(x,k,v,u)=\frac{\sin x}{x^{2}k^{2}}\int_{0}^{x}{\rm}\overline{x}\,\overline{x}\sin(x-\overline{x})f^{i}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\\
&=\frac{\sin^{2}x}{x^{2}k^{2}}\int_{0}^{x}{\rm}\overline{x}\,\overline{x}\cos\overline{x}f^{i}(\overline{x},k,v,u)-\frac{\sin x\cos x}{x^{2}k^{2}}\int_{0}^{x}{\rm}\overline{x}\,\overline{x}\sin\overline{x}f^{i}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\,.\end{split}

(398)

As mentioned previously, we need to take the time average of these kernels, and they encapsulate all the time dependence. Moreover, as is the case for second-order iGWs, we are interested in these waves at present times, that is, when the iGWs are deep inside the horizon and the kernels have stabilised to a constant value. This allows us to take the limit
\eta\rightarrow\infty
or
x\rightarrow\infty
(for some fixed value of
k
). As a result, all remaining time dependence resides in the prefactors of the integrals. We conclude that the second term above does not contribute to the SGWB. Accordingly, what we need to calculate is

x^{2}\overline{\mathcal{I}^{i}(x\rightarrow\infty,k,v,u)}=\frac{1}{2k^{2}}\int_{0}^{x\rightarrow\infty}{\rm}\overline{x}\,\overline{x}\cos\overline{x}f^{i}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\,,

(399)

where we recall the functions
f^{i}(\overline{x},k,v,u)
depend on the source term and are given in Eqs. (
364
), (
365
), (
364
), (
374
), (
385
), and (
394
). We note that Ref.
[
61
]
examines the impact of third-order contributions on the IR part of the iGW power spectrum, whereas here we will examine the impact on the spectral density. The two terms in Eq. (
398
) contribute to the power spectrum, but only the first term contributes to the spectral density. Once again, the kernels and nested kernels involve integrals of products of highly oscillatory and decaying functions, so they will involve the trigonometric integrals
\text{Ci}(z)
and
\text{Si}(z)
defined in Eq. (
216
). As we will see, the nested kernels depend on products of these trigonometric integrals, making it impossible to derive closed-form analytical expressions for terms containing nested kernels. Consequently, we will evaluate such kernels numerically. This is achieved using a fourth-order Runge-Kutta method, where we have tested our code by comparing numerical kernels for SIGWs and scalar-tensor iGWs to the known analytical ones. Finally, we note a key difference from second-order iGWs: the kernels are not squared. As a result, these contributions can be negative, potentially suppressing the signal.

20.1
Kernel computations

In this subsection, we compute the various kernels needed. Firstly, we look at the kernels coming from the pure first-order contribution and then move on to the second-order kernels, which involve numerical computations.

There are three kernels we need to compute for the contribution composed of the scalar-scalar-tensor interactions. With the formalism established, the next step is simply to substitute in Eqs. (
364
), (
365
) and (
366
) into Eq. (
399
). This leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}x^{2}\overline{\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{s,1}(x\rightarrow\infty,k,v)}&=-\frac{1}{315v^{6}}\bigg(v^{2}\left(648+1179v^{2}-739v^{4}\right)+\sqrt{3}v^{3}\big(1890-672v^{2}\\
&+11v^{4}\big)\left|\frac{\sqrt{3}-v}{\sqrt{3}+v}\right|+9\left(216-903v^{2}+280v^{4}\right)\log\left|\frac{v^{2}-3}{3}\right|\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(400a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}x^{2}\overline{\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{s,2}(x\rightarrow\infty,v)}&=\frac{1}{30v^{6}k^{2}}\bigg(3v^{2}\left(6+11v^{2}\right)-2\sqrt{3}v^{3}\left(v^{2}-15\right)\left|\frac{\sqrt{3}-v}{\sqrt{3}+v}\right|\\
&+18\left(3-5v^{2}\right)\log\left|\frac{v^{2}-3}{3}\right|\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(400b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}x^{2}\overline{\mathcal{I}^{ssh}_{s,4}(x\rightarrow\infty,v)}&=\frac{1}{120v^{6}k^{2}}\bigg(6v^{2}\left(62v^{2}-3\right)+\sqrt{3}v^{3}\left(285-13v^{2}\right)\left|\frac{\sqrt{3}-v}{\sqrt{3}+v}\right|\\
&-9\left(6+65v^{2}+5v^{4}\right)\log\left|\frac{v^{2}-3}{3}\right|\bigg)\,.\end{split}

(400c)

We also observe that these kernels remain well-behaved and finite in the limit
v\rightarrow\sqrt{3}
, exhibiting no logarithmic divergence.

We shift our focus to computing the kernels from source terms that couple second-order perturbations, which will involve nested kernels. For second-order scalars coupled to first-order fluctuations, the function we have to integrate over is Eq. (
374
), which depends on the nested kernel defined in Eq. (
376
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)&=\frac{1}{k^{2}v^{3}\overline{x}^{3}}\int_{0}^{v\overline{x}}{\rm}\tilde{y}\,\,\tilde{y}\Bigg(3(-v\overline{x}+\tilde{y})\cos\left(\frac{v\overline{x}-\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)+\sqrt{3}(3+v\overline{x}\tilde{y})\\
&\times\sin\left(\frac{v\overline{x}-\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\Bigg)\Sigma^{st}(\tilde{y},v,u)\,,\end{split}

(401)

in an RD universe. We further split this up as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)=\frac{1}{k^{2}v^{3}\overline{x}^{3}}\Bigg(3\cos\left(\frac{v\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{c2}(\overline{x},v,u)-3v\overline{x}\cos\left(\frac{v\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{c1}(\overline{x},v,u)\\
&-3\sqrt{3}\cos\left(\frac{v\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{s1}(\overline{x},v,u)-\sqrt{3}v\overline{x}\cos\left(\frac{v\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{s2}(\overline{x},v,u)\\
&+3\sqrt{3}\sin\left(\frac{v\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{c1}(\overline{x},v,u)+\sqrt{3}v\overline{x}\sin\left(\frac{v\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{c2}(\overline{x},v,u)\\
&+3\sin\left(\frac{v\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{s2}(\overline{x},v,u)-3v\overline{x}\sin\left(\frac{v\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{s1}(\overline{x},v,u)\Bigg)\,,\end{split}

(402)

where we have defined

\displaystyle I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{c1}(\overline{x},v,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{v\overline{x}}{\rm}\tilde{y}\,\cos\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\tilde{y}\,\Sigma^{st}(\tilde{y},v,u)\,,

(403a)

\displaystyle I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{s1}(\overline{x},v,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{v\overline{x}}{\rm}\tilde{y}\,\sin\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\tilde{y}\,\Sigma^{st}(\tilde{y},v,u)\,,

(403b)

\displaystyle I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{c2}(\overline{x},v,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{v\overline{x}}{\rm}\tilde{y}\,\cos\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\tilde{y}^{2}\,\Sigma^{st}(\tilde{y},v,u)\,,

(403c)

\displaystyle I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{s2}(\overline{x},v,u)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{v\overline{x}}{\rm}\tilde{y}\,\sin\left(\frac{\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\tilde{y}^{2}\,\Sigma^{st}(\tilde{y},v,u)\,.

(403d)

The analytical results for
I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{c1}(\overline{x},v,u)
,
I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{s1}(\overline{x},v,u)
,

f^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{x},k,v,u)
into an analytical part and numerical part:

f^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{x},k,v,u)=f_{a}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{x},k,v,u)+f_{n}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\,,

(404)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{a}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{x},k,v,u)&=\frac{24\sin\overline{x}}{k^{2}u^{4}v^{4}\overline{x}^{7}}\sin\left(\frac{u\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\Big(-\sqrt{3}u\overline{x}(-18+(1+u^{2}-3v^{2})\overline{x}^{2})\cos\left(\frac{u\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\\
&+3(-18+(1+3u^{2}-3v^{2})\overline{x}^{2})\sin\left(\frac{u\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\Big)\,,\end{split}

(405a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{n}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(\overline{x},k,v,u)&=\frac{4}{3u^{3}\bar{x}^{2}}\bigg(6u\bar{x}\cos\left(\frac{u\bar{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)+\sqrt{3}\big(-6+u^{2}\bar{x}^{2}\big)\sin\left(\frac{u\bar{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\bigg)\\
&\times\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)+\frac{4}{\sqrt{3}u\bar{x}}\sin\left(\frac{u\bar{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)I^{\Psi^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\,.\end{split}

(405b)

The analytical contribution, once integrated, is

\displaystyle\begin{split}x^{2}&\overline{\mathcal{I}_{a}^{\Psi^{(2)}\Psi}(x\rightarrow\infty,k,v,u)}=\frac{1}{30k^{4}u^{4}v^{4}}\Bigg(84u^{4}+u^{2}(984-360v^{2})+(828+540u^{2}-540v^{2})\\
&\times\log 3+\big[(-828-540u^{2}+540v^{2}+\sqrt{3}(-630u-80u^{3}+6u^{5}+270uv^{2}-30u^{3}v^{2})\big]\\
&\times\log\left|\sqrt{3}+u\right|+\big[-828-540u^{2}+540v^{2}+\sqrt{3}(630u+80u^{3}-6u^{5}-270uv^{2}\\
&\times+30u^{3}v^{2})\big]\log\left|\sqrt{3}-u\right|\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(406)

For the kernels involving second-order vectors, there is no ‘analytical’ part, and so we do everything numerically. In an RD universe, Eq. (

) becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{\textit{i}}^{B^{(2)\Psi}}(\overline{x},k,v,u)&=\frac{1}{3ku^{3}v^{2}\bar{x}^{4}}\bigg(6u\bar{x}\big(4+v^{2}\bar{x}^{2}\big)\cos\left(\frac{u\bar{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)+\sqrt{3}\big(-24+\bar{x}^{2}\big(-6v^{2}\\

&+\frac{1}{ku^{3}v^{2}\bar{x}^{3}}8\sqrt{3}\sin\left(\frac{u\bar{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)-8u\bar{x}\cos\left(\frac{u\bar{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{x}}I^{B^{(2)}}_{sti}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\,,\end{split}

Finally, for the background generated by second-order tensors coupled to first-order scalars, we can similarly split up the kernel Eq. (

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f_{a}^{h^{(2)\Psi}}(\overline{x},k,v,u)=\frac{48k^{2}\sin\overline{x}}{u^{6}\overline{x}^{9}}\left(u\overline{x}\cos\left(\frac{u\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)-\sqrt{3}\sin\left(\frac{u\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\right)\bigg(u\overline{x}(-18+(-3\\

(408a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f_{n}^{h^{(2)\Psi}}(\overline{x},k,v,u)=\frac{12k^{2}}{u^{3}\overline{x}^{5}}\bigg((u\overline{x}\left(18+(3-u^{2}+9v^{2})\overline{x}^{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{u\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)+3\sqrt{3}\\
&\times\big(-6+(-1+u^{2}-3v^{2})\overline{x}^{2}\big)\sin\left(\frac{u\overline{x}}{\sqrt{3}}\right)\bigg)I^{h^{(2)}}_{st}(\overline{x},k,v,u)\,.\end{split}

(408b)

Substituting
f_{a}^{h^{(2)\Psi}}(\overline{x},k,v,u)
into Eq. (
